# Preface
## To the Public.

If there has ever been a subject that has occupied the pens of scholars of all kinds, and has given rise to such opposite and contradictory judgments, it is certainly the ancient **Order of Freemasons**, which for so many centuries has already spread through many Christian lands. This Order has (as is mostly the case with unknown subjects) passed through good and evil reports, <note>original: "durch gute und böse Gerüchte gegangen" — a phrasing suggesting the Order has survived both praise and slander.</note> in such a way that

# Preface
## to the Public.

**W**henever there has been a subject that has occupied the pens of scholars of all kinds, and given rise to such opposite and contradictory judgments; it is certainly the ancient Order of Freemasons, which has already been spread through many Christian countries for so many centuries. This Order has (as usually happens with unknown subjects) passed through good and evil reports, <note>The author refers to the "fama bona et mala," a common rhetorical device used to describe how a group's reputation fluctuates based on rumor rather than direct knowledge.</note> in such a way that

<gloss>Vorbericht: Preface or Foreword</gloss>
<gloss>Publikum: The Public / Readership</gloss>
<gloss>Gelehrten: Scholars or the Learned</gloss>
<gloss>Urtheilen: Judgments or Opinions</gloss>
<gloss>Orden der Freymaurer: Order of Freemasons</gloss>
<gloss>Gerüchte: Rumors</gloss>

<sig>A 2</sig>
<meta>catchword: some</meta>

<summary>This page begins the author's preface, framing Freemasonry as a long-standing but deeply misunderstood institution that has historically been the subject of conflicting scholarly debate and public rumor.</summary>
<keywords>Freemasonry, Public Opinion, Enlightenment scholarship, Secret Societies, Reputation</keywords>

--- Page 10 ---
<meta>This page continues the preface. The previous page ended by stating that the Order of Freemasons has been subject to both praise and slander, "in such a way that..."</meta>

<image-desc>Three decorative fleurons or floral ornaments arranged horizontally at the top center of the page.</image-desc>

...some have raised it to the very heavens, while others, on the other hand, have pressed it down to the earth.

Just recently, and specifically at the end of last year, the famous and modest Royal Danish Councilor of State, Mr. Johann Jacob Moser <note>Johann Jacob Moser (1701–1785) was a renowned German jurist and a leading authority on the constitutional law of the Holy Roman Empire.</note>, published a small treatise consisting of three sheets <note>A "sheet" (Bogen) was a printer's measure; three sheets would be a pamphlet of roughly 48 pages.</note> under the title: *On the Toleration of Freemason Societies; Particularly in Regard to the Peace of Westphalia*. In this writing, he examines the following four questions:

1) Are Freemason lodges contrary to the Peace of Westphalia <note>The Peace of Westphalia (1648) was the foundational legal treaty of the Holy Roman Empire, which regulated which religious and social groups were legally permitted to exist.</note>, and if so, why?

2) Is a German sovereign <term>Landesherr: the ruling prince or lord of a specific territory</term> obligated to abolish them? or

3) Can he at least abolish them whenever he wishes?

4) Can the estates <term>Landstände: the representative bodies of a territory, usually comprised of the nobility, clergy, and cities</term> make a formal grievance of it if a sovereign tolerates them in his lands?

And it must be confessed that he answers these questions with much caution and moderation.

<summary>The author introduces a legal treatise by Johann Jacob Moser that examines the legality of Freemasonry under the Peace of Westphalia and the rights of German princes to tolerate or abolish the lodges.</summary>
<keywords>Johann Jacob Moser, Peace of Westphalia, Freemasonry, legal status, sovereign rights, estates of the realm, religious tolerance</keywords>

--- Page 11 ---
<meta>This page transitions from the legal analysis of Johann Jacob Moser mentioned on the previous page to the author's personal commission and defense of the Masonic order. The catchword "lichen" at the bottom of the page is the suffix for the word "weltlichen" (worldly/secular), which concludes the sentence on the following page.</meta>

->[Image Description: Three small decorative floral ornaments arranged horizontally at the top of the text block.]<-

Since I now have the honor of being a member of this praiseworthy brotherhood <term>Verbrüderung: a fraternal organization or society</term>, and since it is of great importance to this group to root out prejudices from the hearts of our fellow citizens; my highly honorable and most wise superiors have strictly commissioned me to undertake this important work. I will therefore, as far as my strength allows, most zealously endeavor to convince the public that our pure, clear, and hallowed intentions have no other ultimate goal than: to attain **wisdom, art, and virtue**; to please **God**; to serve our neighbor; and through this laudable occupation, to make both ourselves and our disciples <term>Lehrjünger: apprentices or students within the craft</term> and sons of wisdom <note>A traditional term for initiates into philosophical or esoteric mysteries.</note> into useful and capable citizens of the state, and to instruct them in the associated duties in the most conscientious manner. At the same time, I will seek to clear the triple stumbling block <note>A "stone of stumbling" (based on Romans 9:33), referring here to the three main public criticisms of the order.</note> out of the way, and show:

> a) That the secrecy of our internal constitution and the hieroglyphic teaching method <note>The "hieroglyphic teaching method" refers to the Masonic practice of using symbols and allegories to convey moral lessons.</note>, which we employ in our assemblies, can in no way make us suspicious.

> b) That the oath which we take, and the manner in which we take it, [is contrary] neither to divine nor worldly... <note>The sentence continues on the next page: "...laws."</note>

<summary>The author identifies himself as a Freemason commissioned by his superiors to defend the order's reputation. He outlines his intent to prove that Masonic goals—wisdom, virtue, and good citizenship—are honorable and that their secrets and oaths are harmless.</summary>

<keywords>brotherhood, prejudices, wisdom, art, virtue, citizenship, secrecy, hieroglyphics, oath, apology</keywords>

The text continues from page 5, where the author began listing three specific points (a, b, and c) to clear the "triple stumbling block" or public criticisms of the order.</meta>

->[Image Description: Three small decorative floral ornaments arranged horizontally at the top of the text block.]<-

...contrary to worldly laws, and is therefore blameless and irreproachable; and finally

c) That we, being loyal and obedient citizens <term>Bürger: members of a city or state with full rights and duties</term>, would never think to establish a **state within a state** <note>original: "statum in statu"; a common political anxiety of the time that secret societies might operate as a rival government.</note>, and that the accusation made against us in this regard by some ill-intentioned people is unlawful and unfounded. I therefore request you, most Honorable Brothers! as well as every impartial reader, not to pass a hasty judgment on the present writing, but first to read it through with impartial eyes and consider it attentively; it will then be shown that nothing is contained therein which does not perfectly correspond with the truth.

If I achieve this ultimate purpose, according to the wish of my High Superiors, I shall count myself happy; at the same time, it will afford me invaluable pleasure if the learned Mr. State Councilor Moser <note>Johann Jakob Moser (1701–1785) was a leading German jurist whose work on constitutional law was highly influential during the Enlightenment.</note> should deign to read this treatise <term>Abhandlung: a formal, systematic written discourse</term> of mine and honor it with his approval.

Blessed above all, however, if through this writing the most Gracious of Monarchs, the father of his people, is assured that his best

besten

## To the Reader, Most Highly Venerable and Esteemed According to Their Rank!

[Image Description: A decorative woodcut initial 'E' integrated with floral and scrollwork motifs.]

Before I make a beginning of the present treatise, I
should appropriately allow a historical introduction
concerning the origin, antiquity, and current
condition of our illustrious Order <term>Order: refers here to a fraternal organization, likely the Rosicrucians or Freemasons</term> to precede it:
but since Phoebron Chlun <note>A pseudonym for Baron Gottlieb von Hund or another contemporary Masonic writer; 18th-century occultists often used such aliases</note>, one of our mem-
bers, has already carried out this subject <term>Vorwurf: in this context, the "subject matter" or "topic" rather than a "reproach"</term>
from the original sources themselves in a thorough and
convincing manner in a preface placed before the incomparable
*Compass of the Wise* <note>original: "Compaß der Weisen," a major Rosicrucian text first published in 1779 which attempted to document the history of the movement</note>, which is currently ready for
printing; I will therefore mention only a little of it here, as a
small foretaste:

It is indisputable that the foundation of our [Order], [dedicated]
to the Great Architect of the Universe <note>original: "höchsten Baumeister der Welt," a common Masonic and Deist term for God as the creator of a rational, geometric world</note>, and to the benefit of our fellow

<meta>catchword: man</meta>

He intends to provide only a brief overview of the Order's foundations and its dedication to God and humanity.</summary>
<keywords>Phoebron Chlun, Compass of the Wise, Great Architect of the Universe, secret societies, Masonic history, Rosicrucianism</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the sentence from the previous page: "...and to the benefit of our fellow men." The text transitions from the author's introductory address into a mythic history of secret wisdom, tracing a lineage of "primal knowledge" from Adam through the biblical patriarchs to the Egyptian mysteries and beyond. The catchword "nern" at the bottom of the page suggests the next page begins with a word ending in those letters, likely "Indianern" (Indians) or a similar plural noun.</meta>

->❦ ❁ ❦<-

men, must be sought nowhere else than in the knowledge of nature <term>Naturkänntniß: the understanding of both the physical and spiritual laws governing the world</term> imparted by the generosity of God to Adam, the primal father of the entire human race, and propagated to his children and patriarchal descendants before and after the Deluge <note>The Great Flood of Noah</note>. Through these wise patriarchal masters, this knowledge was brought to Noah, and from him and his descendants communicated to the Egyptians, and from them to all other nations of the inhabited earth.

No one who has learned even a little of the history of this famous people <note>the Egyptians</note> will deny that they were the first who brought the mysteries of the triple light—namely, natural-spiritual magic, the angelic Cabala <term>Kabala: a mystical tradition concerning the nature of God and the universe, which 18th-century occultists synthesized with Christian and scientific thought</term>, and true natural science—into a systematic order. They taught these in their secret assemblies, known under the name of initiations, to those accepted and tested members by means of certain hieroglyphic symbols. This was done so that the unworthy, whom they included under the name of the profane, would be kept from the knowledge of such important things, and all abuse would be prevented.

In such a manner, Moses <margin>(a) Acts of the Apostles 7, 22.</margin> was instructed in all the sciences of the Egyptians, just as Daniel was among the Chaldeans, <margin>(b) Daniel 1, 4. 17.</margin> and the children of the prophets in their schools.

In the times following shortly thereafter, this hieroglyphic way of teaching spread further and further among other nations; for we find among the Ethiopians the Gymnosophists <note>original: "Gymnosophisten," a term used by ancient Greeks to describe "naked philosophers" or yogis of India and Ethiopia known for their asceticism and wisdom</note>, among the Persians the Magi, among the Indians

<summary>This page establishes the "prisca sapientia" or ancient wisdom narrative, claiming that secret sciences like magic and Cabala originated with Adam and were preserved by the Egyptians to be taught to biblical figures like Moses.</summary>
<keywords>Adam, Noah, Egypt, Magic, Cabala, natural science, initiations, hieroglyphs, Moses, Daniel, Chaldeans, Gymnosophists, Persians</keywords>

### To the Highly Honored and Valued Reader, According to Rank!

<image-desc>A smaller decorative woodcut initial frame or vignette on the left side of the first paragraph, featuring a tree and scrollwork.</image-desc>

Before I begin the present treatise, it would be only fitting to provide a historical introduction regarding the origin, antiquity, and current condition of our illustrious Order <term>Order: specifically referring here to the Rosicrucians or a related initiatory society active in the 18th century</term>. However, since Phoebron Chlun <note>a pseudonym used by a member of the Order, often associated with the authorship of "The Compass of the Wise"</note>, one of our members, has already thoroughly and convincingly presented this subject from primary sources in a preface to the incomparable *Compass of the Wise* <term>Compass der Weisen: a significant Rosicrucian and alchemical text first published in 1779 that attempted to synthesize various mystical traditions</term> (which is now ready for printing), I will only mention a small portion of it here as a brief preview.

It is indisputable that the foundation of our [Order], dedicated to the Highest Architect of the World <note>a title for God common in esoteric and Masonic traditions, portraying the Divine as the supreme designer of the universe</note> and to the benefit of our fellow

<summary>In this preface, the author addresses the reader and identifies "The Compass of the Wise" as a primary source for the Order's history. He establishes the Order's mission as being dedicated to God (the "Architect of the World") and the service of humanity.</summary>
<keywords>Preface, Phoebron Chlun, Compass of the Wise, Architect of the World, Rosicrucian history, secret societies</keywords>

---
*Translator Note: The text ends abruptly at "fellow" (Nebenmenschen), continuing on the next page with a discussion of the knowledge of nature and the lineage of wisdom from Adam.*

--- Page 17 ---
<meta>This page continues from page 8, where the author began a brief historical preview of the "Order." The sentence continues from the previous page's discussion on the exercises of the Order being dedicated to the benefit of fellow humanity.</meta>

<image-desc>Three decorative floral/leaf ornaments arranged horizontally at the top of the page.</image-desc>

[man-] kind must be sought nowhere else than in the knowledge of nature <term>knowledge of nature: *Naturkenntniß*, referring to an esoteric understanding of the physical and spiritual laws of the universe</term> communicated by the generosity of God to Adam, the forefather of the entire human race, and passed down to his children and patriarchal descendants both before and after the Deluge. Through these patriarchal wise masters, this same knowledge was brought to Noah; by him and his descendants, it was communicated to the Egyptians, and from them to all other peoples of the inhabited earth. No one who has learned even a small amount regarding the history of this famous people <note>referring to the Egyptians</note> will deny that they were the first to bring the secrets of the threefold light—namely, natural-spiritual magic, the angelic Kabbalah <note>original: *englischen Kabala*; "englisch" here refers to "angelic" (*Engel*) rather than the English nation</note>, and the true science of nature—into a systematic order. They taught these in their secret assemblies, which are known by the name of "initiations," to those accepted and tested members using certain hieroglyphic symbols. This was done so that the unworthy, whom they included under the name of "the profane," would be kept from the knowledge of such important things and all abuse would be prevented. In such a manner, Moses was instructed in all the sciences of the Egyptians <margin>(a) Acts VII, 22.</margin> just as Daniel was among the Chaldeans, <margin>(b) Daniel I, 4. 17.</margin> and the children of the prophets were taught in their schools. In the times that followed shortly thereafter, this hieroglyphic way of teaching spread further and further among other nations; for we find the Gymnosophists <term>Gymnosophists: "naked philosophers," a term used by the Greeks for ancient Indian or Ethiopian ascetics</term> among the Ethiopians, the Magi among the Persians, and among the Indians—

# Part I.

The preliminary introduction has made known in few words what and who we actually are. The impartial world will see from this with little effort that a perfectly irreproachable intention animates our **Order's alliance**, which serves as a significant proof that our **brotherhood** can count within its bosom powerful princes, high and low nobility, military and state officials, and even clergy and scholars from all four **faculties** <note>The four traditional academic divisions of a university: Theology, Law, Medicine, and Philosophy/Arts.</note>; this would certainly not have happened if they were not convinced that this brotherhood of ours teaches nothing against religion, the state, the love of one's neighbor, and against good morals in general; but rather that it contributes much to the **welfare** of the commonweal. ### Point I.

->❀ ❀ ❀<-

...recognized [on mere faith]. <note>The text here completes the thought from the previous page: "We promise no one, neither [upon mere faith...]"</note> The closer knowledge of God through the works of His creation, the radical <note>Literally "root-like" (wurzelmäßig); meaning to understand things from their very origin or essence.</note> unlocking of all created things, and the benefit that accrues to all other sciences from this—this is the object of our pursuits. We seek to make these efforts of ours useful for the collective benefit of the human race; one can therefore, with perfect right, call us <term>Cosmopolitans (original: *Kosmopoliten*); "citizens of the world" who view all of humanity as their fellow citizens</term>, that is, such world-citizens who have the well-being of their fellow men very close to their hearts.

Since it cannot be otherwise than that our brotherhood—having applied itself with combined forces to every imaginable science for so many millennia—must have attained a great certainty; and since it also establishes certain principles and <term>axioms (original: *Axiomen*); self-evident truths that serve as the foundation for further reasoning</term> as its basis, which through many years of experience have attained a nearly mathematical certainty—and yet, the teachings of all four <term>faculties (original: *Facultäten*); the four traditional divisions of the university: Theology, Law, Medicine, and Philosophy</term>, or the so-called main estates of the <term>Republic of Letters (original: *gelehrte Republik*); the international community of intellectuals and scholars</term>, are disfigured with so many obscure, and even dangerous, propositions—it will be very easy to understand that their eradication is not only a desirable thing, but also that no one is better suited for their improvement than the true, genuine, and right <term>Freemasons (original: *Freymäurer*)</term>.

It would therefore be desirable for the good of the commonwealth that those to whom the improvement of academic studies has been officially entrusted would seek to acquaint themselves with our aforementioned and tested principles by actually entering into our blessed society, and seek to convince themselves of their excellence through practical experience.

this <term>erudition (original: *Gelahrheit*); a term for deep scholarship or learning</term>: And if it is not this, but rather only evil intentions that accompany us to the threshold of the Temple of Wisdom, then we stumble onto such wrong paths upon which we rush with swift steps toward our downfall. ...or ascend to civil service. — Indeed, a certain d’Argens <margin>(o)</margin> is not ashamed, to the dishonor of his own intellect, to give the reckless counsel “to establish the fundamental constitution of Europe without any connection to religion.” Could even a horned beast <note>original: *Hornvieh*; a derogatory term for a stupid or unthinking person, usually an ox or cattle</note>, if it could speak, chatter more irrationally?

Now, this is not to say that I believe all modern naturalists are Freethinkers. No, I do not believe this at all; but rather, that the majority of them actually mean to be convinced that the aforementioned system promotes the honor of the most high Architect of the World <note>A common Enlightenment metaphor for God as a master builder or designer.</note> rather than diminishing it. Nevertheless, it remains once and for all certain, that the aforementioned atomistic system—

system may have given the opportunity for some to no longer attribute the origin of the world and its parts to the wise cooperation of God, but rather to a chance confluence of tiny corpuscles <note>original: *Körpergen*; a diminutive form of corpuscle or atom, referring to the "atoms" of the ancient Greeks</note> from which they believe its first foundation consisted. In doing so, they pay no heed to the Creator or to the most highly effective, all-animating Spirit infused by Him into the primal matter <term>Urstoff (original: *Urstoff*); the fundamental substance from which all things are created</term> of things. A noble modern Platonist <note>referring to a follower of the Neoplatonic school of philosophy, which emphasized the spiritual over the material</note> describes it so beautifully that I cannot help but include his words here; they read as follows: "Just as physicians mention a certain disease which they call water-shyness <term>Hydrophobia (original: *Wasserscheue*); the historical term for rabies, characterized by a fear of water</term>, which usually afflicts those wounded by the bite of a rabid dog and causes them to flee most carefully from all water: So too are those who deny the Godhead attacked by a miraculous evil, which one might not improperly call the fear of spirits <note>original: *Geisterscheue*</note>. From this arises the fact that they abhor all spirits and beings freed from coarse, physical matter in a foolish and reprehensible, yet violent manner. Associated with the aforementioned evil is another disease, which is rightly called matter-madness <term>Hylomanie (original: *Hylomanie*); from the Greek *hyle* (matter) and *mania* (madness), describing an obsession with the physical world</term>, whose powerful seizure provokes people to be inflamed with an irrational love for coarse matter, and to sacrifice to it most devoutly, as if it were their only deity." I am well aware that some, particularly the Abbé Vallemont <note>Pierre Le Lorrain, Abbé de Vallemont (1649–1721), who wrote on "occult physics" and dowsing</note>, sought to explain the incident with the dowsing rod of Jacques Aymar <note>original: *Jac. Aymon*; Aymar was a famous French dowser who supposedly used his rod to track a murderer in 1692</note> in France through the principles of corpuscular physics <note>The theory that all natural changes result from the motion and collision of tiny particles or "corpuscles"</note>: however, his proofs are so shallow that this book has brought him little honor. On the other hand, it is quite easy to explain this phenomenon through magnetism and according to our own principles. Therefore, we have banished this adventurous and dangerous theoretical structure from our schools of learning.

(p) This sounds quite different from: ℞. <note>The traditional symbol for "recipe," meaning "Take."</note> White poppy seeds 5 oz., gum arabic, starch, tragacanth, 1.5 drams of each; purslane seeds, cotton seeds, marshmallow, mallow, 1.5 drams of each; the 4 greater cold seeds, husked <note>A traditional medical group consisting of the seeds of cucumber, melon, pumpkin, and gourd.</note>; cleansed quince seeds, 3.5 drams of each; prepared spodium <note>Likely zinc oxide or burnt ivory, used as a drying agent.</note>, licorice juice, 1.5 drams of each; sugar candy to the weight of all; make a powder, labeled: Powder against Consumption <note>original: "Schwindsucht"—a historical term for tuberculosis.</note>. Assuming this, there is also only one and the same matter that stands in an equal relationship with man. The closer this relationship, the more exact is the common agreement.

But where is such a subject to be found that contains this power within itself, and is thus as universal as the first matter itself? For as soon as this has occurred, every creature is transferred from the state of <term>particular specification</term> <note>The state of being a specific, individual thing (like a single plant or metal) rather than the universal essence.</note> into the nature of universality. For in the center of all three components <note>Referring to the "Three Principles" of Salt, Sulfur, and Mercury mentioned on the previous page.</note>, everything is nothing but purity and universality, which is equally essential to all kingdoms of nature.

to preserve them with permanent duration. Now, the Professors of Pharmacy—or, to speak in our German dialect, the master apothecaries—certainly also produce all kinds of essences, tinctures, and so forth from minerals and metals, which they are accustomed to exalt with many praises. However, I have the honor to assure you, though only in all silence and, as it were, *secretly* <note>original: "sub rosa," literally "under the rose," a traditional symbol of secrecy.</note>, that few of these are worth even the coals burned to produce them.

— Why? —

They lack the right <term>philosophical separating knife</term> <note>original: "philosophische Scheidemesser." In alchemy, this refers to a specific solvent or spiritual agent capable of breaking a substance down to its primary components.</note>, with which all metallic and mineral bodies are to be set apart in such a way—without being displaced from their central point—that they can never again be brought back to what they were before (that is, into a metal or mineral), but can only be returned to their primordial essences. 

Therefore, their science goes no further than the preparation of certain antimonial, mercurial, martial <note>Refers to preparations made from iron, associated with the planet Mars.</note>, and other similar preparations. These perform more or less effectively in periodic, alternating diseases <note>Such as intermittent fevers or malaria, which occur in cycles.</note>, depending on whether they were prepared with more or less diligence; however, in chronic cases, they do not perform their duty as reliably as a pure and <term>philosophically reborn</term> metallic-mineral remedy. 

For lack of the aforementioned philosophical separating knife—which is equally essential to all subterranean bodies because they all sprang from it—their "sweetenings" <note>original: "Versüssungen." In chemistry, this is edulcoration (washing away salts), but here it also refers to making harsh mineral medicines palatable.</note> also do not proceed according to their wishes, even though they sometimes use the true subjects from the middle kingdom <note>Likely referring to the vegetable kingdom or an intermediary state of matter in alchemical theory.</note> for that purpose. Thus, they must mostly take their refuge in sugar, honey, syrup, and those pleasant-tasting Borstorf apples <note>A famous historical variety of German dessert apple, used here to mask the bitter or metallic taste of medicines.</note>.

To the latter, however, I say upon my word of honor that our human stomach is no **ostrich stomach** <note>A reference to the folk belief that ostriches could digest iron and other metals; the author is arguing that gold must be chemically "opened" because the human stomach cannot digest it in its raw, metallic state.</note>, which I hope will be sufficient.

These must be brought to the highest purity, liberated from the earthly curse and wrath <note>In alchemical philosophy, "wrath" or the "curse" refers to the impurities and density that matter acquired after the Fall, which the alchemist seeks to purge.</note>, and led into their first spiritual essence—in which the powers of all kingdoms <note>The animal, vegetable, and mineral kingdoms.</note> are to be found. Then, through the cooperation of Nature, it must be transformed into a body shining with heavenly clarity, in which all the fire and light of the entire upper and lower world is indestructibly concentrated into a single point. ### Point III.

By making the love of neighbor one of our primary duties, we seek thereby to revive this virtue, which has almost been extinguished among the greater part of the human race; for we view mankind in their social and natural connection, and imagine them as one and the same person, who consequently is equally concerned with their self-preservation. And since the unchangeable natural law, written in all hearts, obliges us to be mindful of our own self-preservation, it obliges us just as strongly to direct our sympathetic attention toward the self-preservation of our fellow human beings. This truth is supported not only by the Christian religion, the essence of which consists in the love of God and neighbor, but also by the pleasure itself, which

It argues that self-preservation and the care of others are inextricably linked through both natural law and Christian doctrine.</summary>

I [will] not only refute the following three main accusations commonly made against our illustrious **brotherhood** <term>Verbrüderung</term> <note>Likely referring to the Freemasons, a fraternal organization that faced significant scrutiny in the 18th century.</note>, but also prove [the contrary].

## First:

### That the concealment of our internal organization and our hieroglyphic method of instruction cannot make us in the least bit suspicious.

Nothing is more certain, and established in the principles of true **political science** <term>Staatskunde</term>, than that a sovereign is not obliged to tolerate any secret gatherings in his lands without his knowledge and will—especially when he does not know what their actual cause, purpose, and intention is. This is so certain that when, about 29 years ago <note>This places the writing around 1772, referring back to the 1743 raid on the lodge in Vienna by Maria Theresa.</note>, Her Imperial and Royal Apostolic Majesty [Empress Maria Theresa] saw fit to have the Vienna lodge disbanded under many unpleasant circumstances, and some zealous **Masons** <note>Original: *Maçons*. The French term was commonly used in German to refer to Freemasons.</note> complained about it at the table of His Royal Majesty of Prussia [Frederick the Great], this well-known, state-shrewd monarch expressed himself thus: "The Empress is quite right; for, since she cannot know what goes on in the lodges, she is also not obliged to suffer them. I, however, who do know it, cannot only tolerate them, but must also fairly protect and maintain them." And although some, among others the learned Mr. von Sonnenfels (a)

[good] morals would never allow things to fall into disgrace which could influence the public good or cause scandal to our fellow citizens. This is not tolerated among us; we have such means to bind our brothers to both general and specific duties—as required by even the strictest **ethics** <term>Sittenlehre</term> <note>Literally "moral teaching," referring to a rigorous system of conduct.</note>—that we almost never fail to lead erring brothers back to their duty. We are not at all afraid that, through such offended brothers <note>The German phrase "vor den Kopf gestoßen" implies being rebuffed or insulted; here it refers to members who might be angry after being expelled.</note>, anything criminal could come to light, given our blameless conduct.

But, some will object, "how is it then, that you make such a great boast of your sciences; that you employ an impenetrable, **secret method of teaching** <term>Lehrart</term> and such a script that no one can decipher, thereby frustrating its public utility? You consider neither the love of your neighbor and his advantage—which you constantly have on your tongue—nor the ancient philosophical motto which states that every good thing is of such a nature that it should rightfully be shared with everyone (g). It seems, therefore, that your alleged **secrets** <term>Geheimnisse</term> are either of little value, or you must be very malicious and resentful people."

That the sciences which we teach in our secret meetings are not only certain and well-founded, but are also applied to the honor of God and the benefit of the common good—this has already been sufficiently proven in the first part. Indeed, even the **uninitiated** <term>Profane</term> <note>From the Latin "profanus," meaning "outside the temple." In this context, it refers to those who are not members of the Masonic order.</note>, unless they wish to speak against their own better knowledge and conscience, must admit that the writings of Arnaldus de Villa Nova, Basilius Valentinus, Bacon, Paracelsus, van Helmont, and others—all of whom were true **Freemasons**—have already brought great benefit, and could bring even more if one were to begin to value them more highly, read them more diligently, and grasp their true meaning more than has been done until now. I will say nothing of many other magnificent works concerning agriculture, gardening, and domestic economy, mining, brewing, and various other mechanical arts, which were indeed written by brothers, though not as such, but rather under their secular names. For we seek no glory from our efforts, and we do not act as some pompous scholars are accustomed to doing, who place all their titles of honor and name all the learned societies of which they are members immediately after their names on their writings.

However, the fact that we employ a **secret method of teaching** and script in such secret sciences—from which an evil use could easily be made—occurs for important reasons. We are, unfortunately, assured by experience that the close investigation of [nature’s] creatures and their natural **dissection** <term>Zergliederung</term> <note>In the 18th century, this term referred to both chemical analysis (breaking substances down) and anatomical study.</note> can, against our will and intent, easily give many a gold-hungry person cause [to follow] our principles according to the letter...

to understand, and to apply the same to the improvement of base metals <term>Transmutation</term> <note>The alchemical process of attempting to turn base metals like lead into "noble" metals like gold or silver.</note>, but thereby imperceptibly becoming a <term>Laboratory Worker</term> <note>German: *Laborant*. In this period, the term was often used disparagingly for "puffers"—amateur alchemists obsessed only with the physical production of gold.</note> and a <term>Sophist</term> <note>In Masonic and alchemical literature, a "sophist" refers to a charlatan or one who uses deceptive reasoning to justify fraudulent chemical claims.</note>: we therefore employ this caution, so that over time this <term>Laboratory-mania</term> <note>German: *Laborirsucht*. We have a great example in the **Chaldeans**. These were noble **Freemasons**. They devoted themselves primarily to astronomy, astrology, and natural-spiritual magic, and they had brought these arts to a very high level. However, since they were perhaps not cautious enough in the concealment of their dogmas and the protection of their writings, it happened that through the misuse of these works, not only **Chaldea**, but also many other provinces, were as it were flooded with an incredible multitude of soothsayers, interpreters of signs, horoscope-casters, treasure-hunters, and similar vermin; and the true Chaldeans had to bear the shame of their own negligence, seeing these rogues labeled with a title as honorable as the name "Chaldean" once was. So that the like may be prevented in our own times, we make use of the caution mentioned in the present section of this discourse.

How, then, could a mortal endowed with sound reason even dream that men who have voluntarily bound themselves by a solemn vow to constantly practice the fear of God, to perform every conceivable Christian work of charity, and not to grieve their fellow man; to persevere in inviolable loyalty until the end; that, I say, people who are guided by such learned, clever, and godly men—whom God has graced with quite extraordinary wisdom and to whom they have promised complete obedience in all statutes concerning the illustrious **Order**—should overstep the bounds of integrity in such a terrifying and incredible manner, abandon the path of virtue, and suddenly become arch-villains?

> “A vow is generally explained as a voluntary and
> “well-considered promise to God (l) to perform

For the **First**, it is a settled matter of law (n) that anyone who is not under any specific judicial compulsion <term>sui juris: a Latin legal term meaning a person has the full legal capacity to act on their own behalf</term> can make a pledge. Since this is so, and the free, unconstrained will of the person making the vow is unrestricted, true Freemasons can certainly allow both themselves and their candidates to be bound by a vow.

For the **Second**, it requires little reflection to recognize the intention of the true Masonic institution as such an action that is pleasing to God. — Consider for yourself whether the goal of attaining wisdom, art <note>In this context, "art" refers to the "Royal Art," the specialized symbolic knowledge and craft of the Freemasons.</note>, and virtue, of pleasing God, and serving one's neighbor, is not of such a kind that in its practice it leads consistently to the greater glory,

For the Lord alone, who is the first cause, creator, and ruler of all things, is the dispenser of all heavenly and earthly gifts; and only His wisdom imparts to mortal men as much understanding and knowledge of the truth as His all-piercing eye foresees will be useful in this temporal existence to those to whom He wishes to give it. — What a confluence of social duties does our vow not encompass! I wish to briefly summarize these to conclude the present section.

It is, among others, our most prominent duty of the Order: to recognize, praise, fear, and love God the Lord above all things, and to serve Him with all our heart; consequently, to be a true Christian and a friend of religion <note>A term used to denote someone supportive of organized religious faith as opposed to secularism.</note> who is far removed from all freethinking <term>Freethinking (Freygeisterey): A 18th-century term for skepticism or deism that challenged traditional church dogma.</term>.

## Thirdly:

Since we are faithful and obedient members of the state, we would never think of establishing a **state within a state** <note>original Latin: *statum in statu*. This political concept describes a group within a country that operates independently of the national government and its laws.</note>; and the accusation made against us in this regard is unlawful and groundless.

By a **state within a state**, one understands a particular society which, in the midst of the state where it resides, exercises legislative power and a right of coercion over a portion of that state's subjects—a power which belongs to the sovereign alone. (r) — According to this description, a **state within a state** means nothing other than a group of unified subjects who answer to a head other than the local ruler, and who manage among themselves laws different from those established in the state where they live, thus forming a state within another. — In this way was the Society of the **Jesuits** <note>A Catholic religious order. During the mid-to-late 18th century, many European monarchs suppressed the Jesuits, fearing they were more loyal to their own leader (the "Black Pope") and the Vatican than to the nations where they lived.</note> viewed; and it was partly for this reason that the Order—which was so meritorious in the education of youth—was suppressed by secular powers as dangerous. This was because, in both civil and spiritual matters, it was claimed they wished to recognize no other authority than the General of the Order. — However, the hierarchy of the Church must not be misunderstood in this context; its laws are spiritual and bind only the conscience, and its ban <note>referring to excommunication</note> or absolution only harms or benefits those

---

(r) From Bielfeld’s <term>Principles of Political Science</term> <note>original German: *Lehrbegriffe der Staatskunst*. Jacob Friedrich von Bielfeld (1717–1770) was a prominent Enlightenment-era diplomat and political theorist.</note>, Part One, p. 53.

<summary>The author argues that the secret order does not constitute a "state within a state" and differentiates its structure from the controversial Jesuits by noting that its rules are purely spiritual.</summary>
<keywords>sovereignty, state within a state, Jesuits, political science, Bielfeld, church hierarchy, obedience</keywords>

to the souls of the members subject to it. And although the ecclesiastical courts <note>original: *geistliche Gerichte*</note>, primarily in matrimonial matters <note>original: *Ehesachen*</note>, employ a certain degree of punishment and coercion; this occurs not merely out of leniency, but with the express will of the **secular arm** <note>original: *weltlicher Arm*; refers to the civil government’s power to enforce laws, as opposed to the church's spiritual authority</note>, and is a consequence arising from the advocacy, or the right of protection <note>original: *Schutzgerechtigkeit*</note>, of the territorial sovereignty. The same applies when members of the clergy, due to debts or other offenses contrary to their vocation, are subjected to moderate punishments for their improvement; this always occurs with the tacit consent of the high authorities <note>original: *Obrigkeit*</note>. However, as soon as **capital cases** <note>original: *peinliche Fälle*; from the Latin *poena*, referring to serious criminal cases involving corporal or capital punishment</note> arise, the guilty cleric is degraded and handed over to the secular courts. How little such an accusation applies to us will be indisputably clear from the following.

Our **Order** <note>original: *Orden*</note> is a union of people of all ranks, characters, and positions. — We exclude no one, unless we fail at any time to discover in them the sought-after qualities of integrity <note>original: *Redlichkeit*</note>, philanthropy <note>original: *Menschenliebe*; a core Enlightenment value of "love for mankind"</note>, humble curiosity, and a perfectly Christian virtuous conduct <note>original: *Tugendwandel*</note>. — We never release our members from those duties which they owe by nature, or by virtue of their office and profession, to God, the state, and their neighbor: rather, we strive to train them with all our strength so that they may fulfill their obligations all the more effectively. — We claim no monopoly <note>original: *Monopolium*</note> in any state; we encourage everyone who possesses the necessary insight to acquire the art that we practice; and should this perhaps be an illicit trade?

<term>geistliche Gerichte</term>: ecclesiastical courts
<term>Ehesachen</term>: matrimonial matters
<term>weltlicher Arm</term>: secular arm/civil authority
<term>Advocatie / Schutzgerechtigkeit</term>: legal advocacy or the right of a sovereign to protect and oversee corporate bodies
<term>Landesherrschaft</term>: territorial sovereignty
<term>Obrigkeit</term>: the governing authorities
<term>Orden</term>: Order (the secret society)
<term>Redlichkeit</term>: integrity/honesty
<term>Menschenliebe</term>: philanthropy/charity
<term>Tugendwandel</term>: virtuous way of life
<term>Monopolium</term>: monopoly

...be? Has not the investigation of the **works of nature** <note>original: *Naturwerke*</note> been the occupation of individual members of a state since time immemorial? And is it then not enough of an advantage for the state if the success of these experiments is used for its benefit? — 

The rules which our wise masters prescribe to us from time to time are based on the necessary caution to hide ourselves from the lynx-eyes of certain gold-hungry children of men, who represent us as **gold-makers** <note>original: *Goldmacher*; alchemists who claim to transmute base metals into gold</note>, although we make this art nothing less than our main object; rather, we view it at most as a consequence of a closer **knowledge of nature** <note>original: *Naturkenntniß*</note>, which is granted only to very few children of fortune. — 

The laws to which our superiors lead us arise from the ultimate purpose of our **foundation** <note>original: *Stiftung*; referring here to the establishment of the Order</note>; in part, they have as their focus special instruction for the attainment and application of our art, and in part, godly precepts for the practice of Christian and virtuous actions. Is this perhaps called an unlawful legislative power? To this I answer: It would be so, if our laws subjected us to the recognition of any **authority** <note>original: *Obrigkeit*</note> other than that of the state in which we live; or if, beyond such circumstances that flow directly from the constitution of our association, we prescribed orders and statutes. 

It is indeed true that we strive to establish **harmony** <note>original: *Eintracht*</note> and peace among our brothers between whom disputes have occurred in civil life, and to prescribe to them, so to speak, laws for their reconciliation based on natural equity <note>original: *Billigkeit*</note>. But can we then be accused of an unauthorized **right of coercion** <note>original: *Zwangrecht*</note>? Do we behave in such a case in any other manner,

<summary>The author argues that the Order's study of nature benefits the state and clarifies that its internal rules are for spiritual growth and scientific caution, not to usurp government authority. He justifies the Order's role in mediating private disputes between members as an act of promoting peace rather than an exercise of illegal legal power.</summary>

...way, to which natural **philanthropy** <note>original: *Menschenliebe*; literally "love for mankind," a core Enlightenment virtue</note> obligates us? In this, we appear as a pious **head of the household** <term>Hausvater</term> <note>A traditional German concept of the father as the moral and practical governor of a domestic estate.</note>;
and just as no reasonable man can blame such a father if he,
in order to maintain peace and quiet among his family,
establishes various quite specific domestic rules;
likewise, it cannot be reckoned as an unlawful presumption 
on our part if we—since we consider one another as 
**brothers** and our superiors as **fathers**, and consequently 
view ourselves collectively as one and the same family—
bind ourselves to such laws which our wise **masters**, 
as our heads of the household, find appropriate to the 
holiness and propriety of our intentions. I do not 
believe that this can rightly be compared to a power 
which belongs solely to the sovereign of a state. — 
Let one show a single true **Freemason** who, when 
called to account as a citizen for some of his actions, 
has refused to appear before his regular judge, or 
to answer according to the **laws of the land** <note>original: *Landesgesetze*</note>.
But just as no person in the world can show a true 
Freemason who has acted thus, nor will bring forth 
a single example of it; so it remains necessarily and 
irrefutably true that the reproach made against the 
laudable society of genuine Freemasons is unfounded, 
unlawful, and slanderous; **Q.E.D.** <note>original: *w. z. e. w.*, an abbreviation for *was zu erweisen war*, the German equivalent of the Latin *quod erat demonstrandum*, meaning "which was to be demonstrated."</note>

clear; and the impartial reader will draw the conclusion themselves regarding the widespread and very important influence that their existence must have on the **general welfare of states**; but at the same time, in all likelihood, the reader will raise the quite fair question: whether the **Freemasons** <term>Freymäurer</term> <note>At this time, Freemasonry was often under scrutiny by European monarchs who feared secret societies might harbor political subversion.</note> have never deviated from this original purpose of their founding, and consequently whether their labors and social gatherings are still directed toward that end today?

To this, the following serves as true information: That the constitution of **true Freemasonry** <note>The author distinguishes "true" or "genuine" Masonry from other contemporary branches that he may consider illegitimate or deviant.</note> is like a rock, against which all the waves of the world’s oceans may strike, but will never shake. True Freemasons, according to infallible records, trace their foundation back to the **original ancestor of all mankind** <note>original: *Urstammvater*; a reference to Adam, who in Masonic legend was the first to possess divine knowledge of the arts and sciences.</note>; the first establishment of their internal constitution, however, took place three to four thousand years ago among the **priests of Egypt** <note>original: *Priestern Egyptens*; Enlightenment-era thinkers often viewed ancient Egypt as the source of all "Mystery" traditions and hidden wisdom.</note> assembled under their king. 

->❦ ❦ ❦<-

From there, the Order gradually spread to other realms through wise men who had traveled to Egypt to be **initiated** into the mysteries of the priests there—such as, for example, **Zoroaster**, **Orpheus**, **Homer**, **Pythagoras**, and many others. Through **Moses**, these mysteries were brought to the priests and prophets of the Israelite people; through them—by the election of grace of the **Eternal Architect** <term>ewiger Baumeister</term> <note>original: *ewigen Baumeisters*; a common Masonic title for God, often referred to as the "Great Architect of the Universe."</note>—to **Solomon** under **Zadok** and **Nathan**, and subsequently to **Elijah** <note>original: *Eliam*</note>, **Elisha** <note>original: *Elisäum*</note>, and many others. Through others, however, they were gradually made known and propagated among all peoples of the known world under various names and external forms—forms which were very often altered over the course of time as circumstances required, so that

<summary>This page argues for the timeless stability of "true" Freemasonry and traces its legendary lineage from Adam and the priests of ancient Egypt through biblical figures and Greek philosophers.</summary>

...[howev]er, for certain reasons, it carries the name of **true Freemasonry** <term>ächte Freymäurerey</term> and the external constitution associated with it—a name established several **centuries** <note>original: *Säculis*; Latin for centuries or ages.</note> ago and still flourishing to this hour. Internally, however, in an uninterrupted chronological sequence, the labors of its **associates** <term>Bundesverwandten</term> <note>Literally "covenant-kin," referring to the members of the Masonic brotherhood.</note> have at all times been directed, are still really directed, and under the grace of God's blessing will continue to be directed in the future toward the aforementioned ultimate goal.

I am well aware that various subjects mentioned in this treatise will surprise many members of this brilliant **Order** <term>Orden</term>. Some will regard them as strange and unknown matters, while others may view my frankness as a very punishable violation of our **sacred seal** <term>geheiligtes Sigill</term> <note>The "seal" refers to the Masonic oath of secrecy regarding their rituals and internal teachings.</note> and will wish to condemn it; I, on the other hand, hasten to convince the latter of my inviolable brotherly loyalty, and the former of the truth of my discourse. I therefore ask you, **Very Worshipful Brothers!** <term>Ehrwürdige Brüder</term> <note>A formal and respectful title for high-ranking members or the collective assembly of a Lodge.</note> for your kind attention and for the magnanimity of your customary indulgence; for the importance of my subject deserves both, but it exceeds my abilities to present it worthily. Before a **profane** <term>profan</term> <note>In Masonic terminology, "profane" refers to the world outside the Order; it describes those who have not been initiated into the mysteries.</note> judgment seat, art and effort would hardly protect me against a hasty judgment; but before you, the justice of my cause itself, the intent and lawfulness of my writing, and the pure truth of my discourse will surely protect me. The virtues with which you shine, far elevated above all the prejudices of the world, are my guarantee; but above all, that **active love of humanity** <term>thätige Menschenliebe</term> to which we are all so sacredly bound by virtue of our most precious covenant.

The introduction and context of my **treatise** <term>Abhandlung</term> along with the following appendix must be proof enough for you that I have written nothing of my own impulse, but everything at the **command** <term>Anordnung</term> of a higher power, and must make this writing known through printing. Could I allow it to step into the light under my own name, or even a foreign one, without being authorized for it, or being nonsensical? — Judge for yourselves! But, some will think, did the higher **Brothers** <term>Brüder</term> have sufficient right and reason to make our intentions known, which until this hour lay shrouded under the sacred veil of our **secrets** <term>Geheimnisse</term>? Yes, Very Worshipful Brothers! Do not doubt! According to the measure of time and circumstances, prudence changes laws and order. A thousand proofs assure you that not profane <note>original: *profane*; referring to the uninitiated or secular world.</note> wisdom, but indeed true **wisdom** <term>Weisheit</term> guides and leads our higher brothers in all their steps. The point in time—the so important point in time—approaches, where that for which formerly only very few could be chosen, now to many worthy brothers, instead of the appearance, the light itself shall be placed before their eyes; and instead of the one drawn from the grave <note>A reference to the Masonic legend of Hiram Abiff, whose "lost" word is traditionally sought through a symbolic ritual of death and rebirth.</note>, the true **Master's Word** <term>Meisterwort</term>, which was lost for many, shall be placed back into their mouths. For barbarism and superstition now lie on the ground; sound **reason** <term>Vernunft</term> and **moral teaching** <term>Sittenlehre</term> bloom in all places; the reason to hide our intentions is thus shattered. Only our secrets must we keep sacred at all times: they and their secrecy are the essence of our inner **constitution** <term>Verfassung</term>, and this remains unchangeable. Therefore, while prudence formerly compelled us to keep our intentions carefully hidden, now she advises us to [reveal] them...

<summary>The author argues that his decision to publish Masonic intentions is guided by a higher power and the changing "spirit of the age," which favors reason over superstition. That the purpose of their covenant and their labors consisted of this is even seen by the connoisseurs of their hieroglyphs <note>In the 18th century, "hieroglyphs" often referred to symbolic alchemical or Masonic emblems rather than Egyptian script.</note> on the remains of their ancient monuments; and who does not know of the levels of those hard trials that everyone had to go through before they could join their inner secrets? Even the kings themselves, in order to take part, had to undergo them.

These trials, however, were arranged at the very first establishment of the internal constitution of our Order <term>Orden</term>, and divided into stages, or degrees <term>Grade</term>. They are still maintained, and indeed always with the wisest intention: to entwine our secrets with the greatest caution and to seal them under an impenetrable cover of secrecy; but at the same time, to investigate at the beginning the candidate’s <term>Aufnähmlinge</term> curiosity, flexibility, and other mental faculties, and so, gradually, to lead them to that part of our labors which fits their natural disposition.

-> ❦ ❦ ❦ <-

...sound very contradictory, if a manifold experience did not confirm it.

Whoever merely considers (without even looking at the happiness that every brother can enjoy within the interior of the Order and extend to his fellow human beings) the advantages a Freemason has for himself over all the profane <term>profane</term> <note>In Masonic terminology, "profane" refers to those who are not initiated into the Order—from the Latin "pro fano," meaning "outside the temple."</note> in the world, must already be perfectly convinced of the merit and utility of the foundation and the expansion of this brilliant Order. 

In a true Freemason whom I meet for the very first time in my life, I have sufficient rationally founded reasons to entrust myself entirely—to be his friend and to support all his endeavors according to my means and reputation. This is because I know that he is my brother and that he knows, practices, and loves the laws of true friendship, integrity, and philanthropy <term>Menschenliebe</term> <note>Literally "love of humanity," this was a core value of the Enlightenment, emphasizing benevolent action and universal brotherhood.</note>; and that members of the Order who distinguish themselves by wickedness are expelled from our midst and made known to all our assemblies. 

Hence it is seen—and it has become a proverb—that for some people everything succeeds in all places; while others, who possess every talent, can make no progress anywhere. 

For there are Masons in all places; they intercede for their brother, and they can do so because they know him. But they cannot do so for the stranger; prudence and philanthropy forbid it to them because they do not know him. Yet it is forbidden only until they are certain of his true merits. But where are there equal paths to prove oneself convincingly without such guidance?

The works of the great pagans <term>Heiden</term> <note>In this context, the author refers to the classical philosophers of Greece and Rome, who were "heathen" or non-Christian but respected as the foundation of wisdom.</note> are the textbooks and the most worthy object of veneration even in our own times. Monasteries were not yet founded at that time, and they would have been even less secure, as all libraries, books, and scholars were sought out from every corner accessible to secular power and destroyed.

<meta>This page continues the argument regarding the Order’s relationship with the state. The previous page (92) ended with a discussion on the Order's secrecy and its respect for sovereigns. This page begins by completing the concept of living in "uniformity" or "conformity" (Gleichförmigkeit) with civil law, demonstrating that the Order is a law-abiding body rather than a subversive one.</meta>

<page-num>93</page-num>

[...live in] conformity with the laws of the country in which he resides; if he knowingly transgresses one, the punishment of excommunication <term>Bannstrafe</term> <note>original: "Bannstrafe" — in this context, a formal expulsion or spiritual ban from the Order, mirroring the severity of religious excommunication.</note> from the Order awaits him. For this very reason, we hold no social meetings of the Order where the legislative power forbids them to us. We also heal no sick person where it is not permitted for us. All that we can do in such a case consists of occasionally making known genuine home remedies, which all professors of public medical science <term>Arzneygelehrsamkeit</term> <note>The formal, academic study and teaching of medicine at a university level.</note> can examine. In a word! we can be of much, very much use to a state; but to cause harm anywhere is incompatible with our inner constitution. 

Therefore, the brothers imprisoned in Naples—who gave occasion for the mentioned writing on the tolerance of Freemason societies—have, according to various public reports, been acquitted on the grounds of innocence <note>original: "ex capite innocentiæ" — a Latin legal phrase meaning 'on the basis of innocence' or 'by reason of innocence.'</note>; conversely, legal proceedings will now be initiated against their accusers. From which it is proven that this imprisonment was based neither on a true crime against the state nor on Italian mistrust, but merely on the personal thirst for revenge of the accusers, which is nothing rare in Italy. 

That the Regensburg Lodge is also still in its full bloom <term>Flor</term> <note>A metaphorical expression meaning at the height of its prosperity or success.</note> and is growing is known to the world. Both incidents, therefore, do not point against, but rather for the praiseworthiness of the Freemason societies. The same follows from what has been cited regarding Benedict XIV <note>The original text uses the spelling "Benedice XIV." Pope Benedict XIV (reigned 1740–1758) was known as a great scholar; while he confirmed the ban on Freemasonry, he was often seen as a moderate and intellectual figure.</note>. ...be indisputably convinced of the evident truth: 1) That all genuine Freemasons <term>Freymäurer</term> <note>The author uses the term "genuine" to distinguish his specific esoteric branch of Masonry from the more common social clubs of the era.</note> of the existing Christian religion, in its full scope, are—regardless of the current state of religious affairs among all Christian parties <note>original: "Parthien." these excellent divine gifts may become known and common to all in every place; for this is the wish and will of our confederacy <term>Bundesgenossenschaft</term> <note>This term refers to the "Fellowship" or "Union" of the Rosicrucian Order.</note>.

And it is not unknown to you how our Order has propagated itself for several thousand years—sometimes publicly, sometimes in secret—depending on whether the Lord of Hosts <note>original: "Herr aller Heerschaaren," a biblical title for God (Dominus Sabaoth), emphasizing His supreme authority over all powers.</note>, to bless or punish the nations, granted them wise or blinded rulers. It has continued under various names down to our own time, ever occupied in exploring the hidden secrets of nature <term>Geheimnisse der Natur</term> in perfect harmony for the general welfare of the world, and communicating from time to time the most useful discoveries to it—to which we owe, if not all, then at least very many of the comforts of human life. This very occupation must always and forever remain a sacred duty for us.

Therefore, dearest brother, you must not wonder when we remind you how greatly the Adversary of God <note>original: "Widersacher Gottes," referring to Satan or the demonic opposition to divine work.</note>, out of malice toward the humanity he loathes, strives to sever our bond through the barbarism, ignorance, self-conceit, arrogance, pride, envy, and greed of his wicked children; and (though this is impossible for him, and shall remain impossible until the end of the world, for the Lord of Hosts himself holds His fatherly hand firmly over it), at least to not only persecute our active associates <term>Bundesverwandten</term> in all places, but also to suppress our most beneficial writings the moment they are made public.

Because he has now, alas! succeeded in this endeavor not infrequently; yes, even many a brother—whom the burning zeal of his philanthropy <term>Menschenliebe</term> <note>Literally "love of humanity," this was a central tenet of Enlightenment-era secret societies who believed their scientific and spiritual discoveries should eventually benefit all people.</note> against the snares of the godless had made less cautious for his own safety than he perhaps should have been—was betrayed by his own writings, or even by the frequent charitable deeds with which he was busy assisting the infirm and the needy; but envy and greed brought him into persecution, prison, yes, even onto the torture rack itself, and finally, after his most sacred duty of the highest secrecy <term>Verschwiegenheit</term> could not be defeated <note>Meaning the brother refused to break his vow of silence even under torture.</note>, they utterly wore him down; therefore, we intentionally mention this sad truth, Dearest Brother! so that you may proceed with a more measured caution in following your commission, but at the same time not lose sight of what almost always tends to happen to our writings intended for the general welfare to be printed.

For some envious, resentful, and selfish people have mutilated and falsified [our works] right at the press; while others, which a special precaution has saved from this mistreatment, have had all copies bought up and carried away at once, and thus withheld from the public <note>original: "Publico," from the Latin 'publicum'.</note>; against the rest, however, which escaped their malice <term>Arglist</term> so far as to actually come to light in a pure form, they have summoned slander, superstition, and all the prejudices of the world to make them suspicious to good-hearted readers. And thus, very many secrets of nature <term>Naturgeheimniße</term>, as important as they are beneficial to the common good, which brothers of our circle have communicated to the world out of philanthropy, have fallen into oblivion after becoming the prey of the malice of individual misers.

We, on the other hand, now proceed to the matter itself, but must first mention in passing that beneath the moon, above, upon, and within the earth, there is no type of body to be found (whether they be known or unknown to our eyes) which our wise masters have not naturally and perfectly examined, and found them in their innermost essence to be so many sources of God's infinite goodness, through which the creative wisdom of the Eternal Almighty allows the most glorious treasures of His bottomless <term>grundlosen</term> <note>In this context, 'grundlos' implies an abyss of grace—unfathomable or without end—rather than the modern meaning of 'without reason.'</note> grace to flow forth, in order to assist man, who has fallen through sin, once again. ...one takes refuge, the true <term>natural fire</term> <note>In alchemy, "natural fire" is a subtle, internal heat that drives growth and transformation, distinct from the destructive heat of a common furnace.</note> is to be found: every matter already contains it within and by itself. It is only the responsibility of the <term>artist</term> <note>The alchemist or practitioner of the Hermetic arts.</note> to know how to adapt it so that it may be awakened and perform its work.

How do the vintner, to prepare wine, and the maltster, to prepare beer, go about it? Does the latter hurry with his grain, and the former with his grapes, immediately to an external fire's heat? Not at all! Both would ruin everything.

The vintner presses the ripe grapes and leaves his <term>must</term> <note>Freshly crushed grape juice that still contains the skins and seeds.</note> to kind Nature in a cool place. She makes use of her own fire—that is, her active spirit—which is hidden within the must. This spirit begins gradually to move, to heat up through movement, and through this heat to excite a fermentation, by which both the wild volatile and the earthly, coarse, and solid impurities are visibly separated from its pure essence. In clean barrels, the clarified liquid is brought into the cellar, where the same worker <note>Referring back to the "active spirit" or "fire" of Nature.</note> continues to separate the impure from the pure, until it is freed from all coarseness <note>original: "Unart" — literally "bad nature," referring to the undesirable qualities or impurities of the raw material.</note> and brought to the perfection of a genuine wine. Any external warmth—no matter when it might be applied—would disturb the inner fire of nature in its operation and, by the very nature of things, ruin everything.

The maltster, on the other hand, initially places his grain in the <term>steeping-cistern</term> <note>original: "Weichstock" — a tank used in brewing for soaking grain to trigger germination.</note> under water until it has swollen and absorbed enough moisture; he then drains the water

The manual operations which the maltster must use for brewing beer are thus very different from those of the aforementioned vintner, even though the materials used by both belong to one and the same kingdom of nature <note>The vegetable kingdom.</note>. This fundamental truth reveals various secrets of nature to the observant researcher, such as why a young bird can no longer hatch from a brooded egg once it has become too cold. This same condition applies to the subjects of all the **kingdoms of nature** <term>Naturreiche</term> <note>Refers to the traditional division of the world into the Animal, Vegetable, and Mineral kingdoms.</note>; it is for this very fundamental reason that various monstrous or "adventurous" births are produced from time to time.

<meta>This page continues the author’s polemic against "charcoal-burners" (pseudo-alchemists) who ignore the laws of nature. It transitions from a philosophical warning into a practical description of the brewing process, contrasting the "artificial" preparation of beer with the "natural" fermentation of wine must.</meta>

...[follow] at least once the certain tracks of wise nature! cry out all true masters; however, people intentionally wish to remain blind with seeing eyes and deaf with open ears. Patience!

If one now wishes to brew beer from the prepared malt, it must be sprinkled and well crushed in the mill, so that its **essential power** <term>Kraftwesen</term> can be easily and completely extracted from the husks or spent grains. Afterward, pure water is boiled and poured into the mashing vats, the crushed malt is mixed into it, and thus the so-called **mash** <term>Meesch</term> <note>A mixture of ground grain and water that is heated to release sugars.</note> is made; this is then placed into the brewing copper, boiled with constant stirring, and worked back and forth. The longer and more diligently this is done, the more perfect the beer will be afterward. The boiled mash is then drained into vats and is now called the **wort** <term>Würze</term> <note>The liquid extracted from the mashing process before it is fermented into beer.</note>. Meanwhile, hops are boiled and, according to the proper order, combined with it; the clear mass is cooled until lukewarm on the cooling frames and left to stand; it is then brought into vats or barrels, pitched with good brewer's yeast, and once again left to nature to complete the required **fermentation** <term>Gährung</term>.

The vintner knows nothing of all these manual tasks. The spirit in the **must** <term>Moste</term> <note>Freshly pressed grape juice that contains the skins, seeds, and stems.</note> has enough strength and watery moisture to achieve its purpose. Nor can its innate power be increased by any artifice; for nature has not provided it with any seed-power for the reproduction of its own kind, as it has the grains, through which their spirit in...

The cause of this lies in the wild, raw, volatile, and styptic-earthy impurities <note>original: *Unarten*, referring to the crude or "ill-behaved" qualities of the raw matter</note>, with which the true essential substance of the wine in the must and of the beer in the wort is entangled and bound, and which are utterly contrary to the vital spirits and other nutritional spirits of man. The must, for example, can be thickened over external heat into a syrup <term>Sapa</term> <note>A traditional grape concentrate made by boiling down unfermented must.</note> without losing anything of its essence other than a tasteless water—which its vapors, if one captures them, undeniably prove. <meta>This page continues the argument from page 124, which contrasted the shallow observations of modern physicists with the profound, nature-based insights of ancient masters. It transitions from a defense of ancient wisdom to a reflection on the persecution faced by the secret society.</meta>

...but even one single great invention of the ancients would outweigh them. This is true both in regard to its utility for human use and in regard to its strength, clarity, and the accuracy of insight into the inner nature of things—which must be the foundation of every genuine invention. By comparison, modern insights are outweighed a thousandfold <note>original: "himmelhoch" (sky-high). The author uses this superlative to emphasize the infinite superiority of ancient Hermetic knowledge over modern science.</note>. Furthermore, scholars are well aware that antiquity possessed many important secrets that remain unknown to the world to this day. Only among us are they still preserved, uncorrupted and carefully guarded.

If this is the case, why then does it happen that the spirit of persecution <term>Verfolgungsgeist: a systematic hostility or zeal for hunting down those with dissenting views, often used in the context of religious or intellectual inquisitions</term> from the masses <note>original: "großen Haufens" (the great heap). This dismissive term refers to the uninitiated public or the "profane" world.</note> attacks and mistreats us and our teachings in the most misanthropic and unloving way, with such relentless violence? We are even forced to live in darkness, sequestered from the world; and as sorrowful as this situation may appear to many of the unholy <note>original: "Unheiligen". In this context, it refers to those who have not been initiated into the order's sacred mysteries.</note>, our only bitter lament is that almost every path is cut off for us to serve our fellow humans <term>Nebenmenschen: fellow men or neighbors</term>, according to our means and strength, without hindrance.

At this point, beloved reader, allow us to turn our word away from you for a few moments and address our disciples <note>original: "Jünger". The text shifts from a public defense to an internal exhortation to the members of the secret society.</note>.

Dearest, most esteemed brothers! Let not the spirit of sadness chain your hearts! Do not become faint-hearted as the unholy are during persecutions! When we closely examine the cause, and who it is that persecutes us, you must rejoice that we have been found worthy to suffer persecution for the sake of the Lord’s name.

For he who does not serve divine wisdom cannot be a truly great man. And how could such a man hate or despise those who, through a holy covenant, dedicate themselves entirely to her <note>Wisdom is personified here as a female figure, likely referring to the tradition of "Sophia" or Divine Wisdom.</note> as servants? 

But pride and self-love—those spawns of Belial <term>Belial: a biblical term for a demon or personification of worthlessness and lawlessness</term>—arouse envy and jealousy in the small and mediocre spirits of the masses, which in turn breeds hatred and persecution against us. Like spiders, they lure and dazzle with the artful web of their writings to ensnare and poison the unwary.

They contain a practical, proven knowledge of hidden nature; their ultimate goal, however, is the true knowledge of the Creator. Remember that the true mark of the wise man is this: that no misfortune crushes him to the ground. God holds him upright in misfortune and gives him strength in his innocence to thank the Lord for His trials, and to be joyful in Him.
