Now, however, beloved reader; let us return to our purpose, in order to explain, firstly, the essential difference between **meteors** <term>Meteoren: in early modern science, this referred to all atmospheric phenomena including rain, dew, snow, and lightning, not just falling stars</term> and the **three kingdoms of nature**, and thereafter also to summarily explain the particular nature and essence of our **Subject** <note>In alchemy, the "Subject" refers to the raw material or "Prime Matter" used to begin the laboratory process</note>, so that above all the basis of our preparation might be clear to every true investigator of nature. What the meteors are, and where and how nature produces them, our masters teach in various printed writings, to which we wish to refer the dear reader in order to avoid all long-windedness. 

Furthermore, however, we have already proven that they hold all four elements contained within a stable mixture, and in this regard resemble other natural bodies. They differ from all others, however, in that 1) nature produces them in the air, and through them sends to its three kingdoms—and most noticeably to the **plant kingdom**—nourishment, strength, and especially that **salt-sulfur spirit** <term>Salzschwefelgeist: a vital, energetic principle believed to be carried by the air and rain</term> mentioned above, as the **vehicle** <term>Vehiculum: a carrier or medium for a subtler, spiritual substance</term> of their universal **seed-power** <term>Saamenkraft: the generative life-force inherent in matter</term>; for which reason they 2) immediately unite themselves with any kingdom of nature, and also instantly take on, receive, and retain the impression and character of that same kingdom and individual body with which they have once combined; because 3) their three essential components have received from nature only the lowest or first **degree of coagulation** <term>Gerinnungsgrad: the state of solidification or thickening from a fluid or vaporous state</term> in their generation, and, as our sages are accustomed to say, are enclosed in the **sphere of Saturn** <term>Sphæra Saturni: an alchemical metaphor for the most crude, dark, or initial state of matter, often associated with lead or the "blackness" of the beginning phase</term>. For which reason their active spirit, caught in its **descent** <note>original: "descensu," referring to the downward movement of celestial influence or physical rain toward the earth</note>, aims for the coagulation of its passive object, and [it affects] the natural bodies of the three kingdoms of nature

The manner of this matter’s birth thus testifies unerringly to the great excellence of its inner nature.

> "It is primarily to be noted here that this substance should never be heated too strongly at the end, but only quite gently, yet it must nonetheless be driven to dryness every time; for otherwise it would eventually ignite and explode <note>original: "fulminiren"</note> like fulminating gold <term>Schlaggold: a highly volatile alchemical gold compound that explodes when heated or struck; the author warns that the finished medicine is extremely potent and reactive</term>."

"The dose is 3 grains <term>Gran: a historical unit of weight approximately equal to 0.06 grams, often compared to the weight of a single grain of wheat</term>; that is, one gives the patient no more than three grains at once, or the weight of 3 peppercorns. What this diet consists of is known to the whole world. Yet, for those who are entirely ignorant, we hereby say: that during and after the use of this medicine, the patient should—at least for a time—abstain from all intrusive emotional disturbances <note>original: "Gemüthsregungen"</note>, harmful air, food, and drink; on the other hand, a true state of mind—"

“ ...peace of mind, as much fresh air as is feasible, and the enjoyment of beneficial food and drink with the required moderation. If these occur, it is a very good sign. The patient is not weakened or debilitated by this, as occurs with other <term>diaphoretics</term> <note>original: "Diaphoreticis"; medicines used to induce sweating to break a fever</note>, but will instead become cheerful and noticeably strengthened in his powers. This is an infallible sign of the harmfulness or goodness of a sweat-inducing remedy. It also effects other excellent purifications through the urine, bowel movements, expectoration <note>phlegm or mucus coughed up from the lungs</note>, and the like, according to the requirements of the patient’s condition. What it further effects imperceptibly in the innermost parts of the body will indeed be faithfully explained below; however, it is not so easily grasped by everyone, though it will be tangibly felt through the results of genuine experience. “

Women who—every time they became pregnant—either <term>miscarried</term> <note>original: "abortirten"</note> or were afflicted with life-threatening labor pains and could never recover a living fruit <note>a historical term for a fetus or newborn</note>, have since then, without any hindrance to their pregnancy, given birth quite easily. They have brought healthy and well-formed children into the world, provided that they took only a single dose of this magnificent medicine in the third month after conception, at the midpoint of their term, and finally at the onset of labor pains. If, as the saying goes, the <term>heart-blood begins to flow</term> <note>original: "Herzgeblüt gehend"; likely referring to severe heart palpitations or internal hemorrhaging, which were often fatal complications during or after childbirth</note>, which is considered a deadly accident for mothers, then...

<meta>This page transitions from the specific medical applications of the remedy (treating childbirth complications and "anonymous" diseases) to a philosophical and alchemical explanation of its source. It attributes the remedy's power to the "food of life" found within the air itself, moving from practical medicine into natural philosophy.</meta>

-><image-desc>Three decorative floral/sunburst woodcut ornaments arranged horizontally at the top center of the page.</image-desc><-

Let us praise God, thank His infinite goodness, and certainly not wonder that so many and great healing powers are concentrated in this <term>substance</term> <note>original: "Materie"; refers to the physical material used to create the remedy</note>. According to His infinite wisdom, the Lord has hidden the most magnificent gifts of His mercy within the most despised things for the humble, so that the proud might not perceive them; for their thoughts do not incline toward such things, nor toward God, but rather they lose themselves in vain, imaginary, high-minded things. We write nothing except that which long experience has perfectly confirmed for us. A true <term>student of nature</term> <note>original: "Naturkenner"; one who understands the secrets of the natural world, similar to a natural philosopher</note> will perceive for himself the great excellence of this substance as soon as he has closely examined and maturely considered its birth and hidden nature.

Our wise <term>Cosmopolite</term> <note>original: "Cosmopolit"; the pseudonym of a famous alchemist, likely Michael Sendivogius (1566–1636), whose works were central to early modern chemistry</note> says: "In the air there is a hidden food of life, which by night we call dew, but by day a spread-out water, whose invisible spirit in congealed form is better than the whole earth." Through the astral spirits of light, or emanations, with which the air is filled, impregnated, and <term>tinctured</term> <note>original: "tingiret"; an alchemical term meaning to imbue a substance with a specific quality or essence</note>, the entire plant kingdom lives: because these spirits are partly attracted by the plants themselves, and partly brought to them through <term>meteors</term> <note>original: "Meteoren"; in this historical context, "meteors" refers to any atmospheric phenomena, such as rain, snow, or dew, rather than objects from space</note>. Through them lives man and the entire kingdom of animals; for we draw them to us through the air and breathe them in with the same. Without them, no one can live or exist. Just as a fire in a stove is smothered without them, so too the flame of life within us is extinguished as soon as we are deprived of air. Some indeed claim: that the air separates from wood, coals, and other combustible things the smoke, vapor, soot, and all

This vital spirit, carried by dew and weather, is presented as the essential source of life for plants, animals, and humans alike.</summary>

--- Page 172 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion from page 143 regarding the "food of life" hidden in the air. The previous page ended by describing how the air is "tinctured" with astral spirits; this page begins by addressing the common (but in the author's view, incorrect) belief that air's only role in combustion is to carry away smoke and waste.</meta>

-><image-desc>Three decorative floral/sunburst ornaments arranged horizontally at the top of the text block.</image-desc><-

...all those parts that dissolve during burning, but which hinder this effect as soon as they are not continuously separated from it; and this is supposedly the only reason why fire can be governed by air, and must be extinguished in its absence. But who would be so dull of mind as not to perceive the baselessness of this error? Smoke and steam hinder burning, and the draft of air relieves the burning <term>matter</term> <note>original: "Materie"; the physical fuel or substance being burned</note> of them; that is perfectly correct. However, in this matter, that is far from being the primary effect of the air, but merely a secondary one; nor is it the sole cause of why fire must be extinguished in its absence. From where does the drawing of air arise through fire? During great <term>conflagrations</term> <note>original: "Feuersbrünste"; large, destructive fires</note>, violent winds are stirred up, even when not a breeze was blowing around them before. As long as it is dark within the furnace, one notices no air; but its draft arises as soon as it burns, and grows according to the measure of the flames and the heat. It also acts most powerfully when everything burns bright as day, and no separation of smoke and steam is any longer perceptible. Cracked stoves fill rooms with smoke as long as the fire burns <term>dimly</term> <note>original: "tumper"; an archaic term describing a smoldering, smoky fire that lacks sufficient air or heat to flame properly</note>; on the other hand, as soon as everything lies in bright, full flames, the smoke in the room disappears. We ask then once more: From where does the drawing of air arise through fire? Here we must touch upon a part of our <term>secret natural philosophy</term> <note>original: "geheimen Naturlehre"; the esoteric study of nature's hidden workings, often linked to alchemy and the belief that spiritual forces govern physical matter</note> that is as important as it is essential, which the eternal, infinitely merciful God—out of wise providence—[has withheld] from the "spirit of invention" for new truths that torments and puffs up the modern legislators in the realm of scholarship...

For his invisible **flame of life** <term>Lebensflamme: A common metaphor in Vitalist medicine suggesting that life is a literal fire within the heart or blood that requires "fuel" and "ventilation" to stay lit.</term>, this **astral air-food** <term>Astralische Luftspeise: The belief that the air contains subtle, celestial nutrients (sometimes called "manna" or "pneuma") that sustain the spirit directly, separate from physical food.</term> is what the above-explained draft of air is; on the other hand, the nutritional juices—cooked into a fine state from food and drink through the first and subsequent **digestions** <note>original: "Dauungen," an archaic form of *Verdauungen*. Early medicine taught that there were three stages of digestion: in the stomach, in the liver/blood, and in the tissues.</note>—are to it what the flammable matter is to a visible burning fire. In his **vital spirits** <note>original: "Lebens, und übrigen Geister"; these were thought to be vaporous substances that carried life and thought throughout the body.</note> and other spirits, however, which arise in part from the said nutritional juices and which are always full of them, there is the **magnet** <note>The "Magnet" here refers to an attractive occult force that draws like-to-like, a central concept in Paracelsian medicine.</note> which attracts the aforementioned astral air-food, because they are of one and the same origin with it, and are for the most part born from it.

Therefore, the air makes manifold impressions on a person's **state of health**. Thus, even infirm people recover some strength during a time of **balsamic air** <note>Balsamic air refers to air perceived as healing, soothing, and restorative, often associated with specific seasons or aromatic environments.</note>, whereas healthy bodies succumb as soon as it is completely corrupted. Sudden anxiety, exhaustion, listlessness, or conversely, courage, power, strength, and cheerfulness are felt by many according to the various changes that occur in the air. If the spirits are in the perfection of their true **temperament** <term>Temperament: The proportional balance of qualities (hot, cold, moist, dry) that determines a person's health and personality.</term>, they attract what is similar to them and repel what is adverse; for this reason, altered air or even **contagious plagues** <note>original: "Seuchen"</note> can make a very small impression on a perfectly healthy, orderly person. But as soon as his spirits are displaced from the sphere of their perfection—whether by an external accident, or by deficient, indigestible, poisonous, or immoderate food and drink, or in whatever other way—they then attract, according to the proportion, degree, and kind of the adverse [impression received...]

<summary>This page explains the physiological theory that the human body "eats" air through the lungs and skin to fuel an internal "flame of life," using a magnet-like attraction to draw in celestial nutrients. It further describes how the quality of the air directly influences mental and physical health, particularly when a person's internal "spirits" are out of balance.</summary>

<keywords>astral air-food, vital spirits, flame of life, digestion, balsamic air, temperament, contagion, medical magnetism, pores</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the discussion on how the human body interacts with the atmosphere. The catchword from the previous page, "somit" (consequently), leads into the description of how the body's internal state reacts to external forces.</meta>

...consequently of a [migh]ty impression <note>The German text begins with the suffix "-tigen," likely the end of "gewaltigen" (mighty/powerful) or "bösartigen" (malignant).</note>, adverse astral influences of the air itself, as often as these may have an influx according to the various changes that occur in the air from time to time. Indeed, the spirits of man sometimes receive an unnatural impression of such a degree that, like a ferment, they darken all nourishment from food and drink, and even the most noble rays of light from the best astral influences of the air, and transform them—according to their unnatural nature, so to speak—into a deadly poison.

And for this reason, at certain seasons, certain diseases prevail among humans and livestock; just as with many sick people, even the most noble air remains harmful or at least unprofitable despite all its balsamic healing power. In this latter case, death must necessarily follow unless counsel is provided in good time through such remedies that penetrate and act not only into the stomach for digestion, <term>Chylification: The process of turning food into chyle, the milky fluid of nutrients that the body can absorb.</term>, and <term>to the primary passages: Original Latin: *ad primas vias*. This refers to the first stage of the digestive system, including the stomach and intestines.</term>, but through the improvement of the spirits themselves into the innermost places of digestion of all parts of the human body.

For this very same reason, we often notice and wonder—yet at the same time quite clearly understand—how many a sick person, though they take little or no food or drink for many weeks, nonetheless casts out such an enormous amount of varied filth through the various <term>excretory organs: Original Latin: *emunctoria*. In early medicine, these were the channels by which the body expelled "superfluities" or waste, such as the sweat glands, kidneys, or bowels.</term>, which the narrow confines of the body could not possibly have contained within itself before the illness. For the malignant impressions of the internal parts of the sick person attract various fluids from the air into themselves at the beginning and during the course of the illness. These internal parts radiate and transform these fluids like a ferment, according to the nature and degree of the <term>malignancy: original German *Malignität*. In this context, it refers to the harmful or "wicked" quality of a disease that corrupts the body’s health.</term> of their received impression. The <term>Archeus: In the medical tradition of Paracelsus, the *Archeus* is the internal "alchemist" or vital force that governs the body’s functions, separating nourishment from waste.</term>, however, strives to separate and sweep these out until it either completely succumbs under the weight of the malignancy...

...consequently, they <note>The "spirits" or vital forces discussed previously.</note> cease their natural functions, or are even driven toward unnatural ones, and eventually death must occur; or else, the evil influences are extinguished by a healing counter-impression, and consequently all adverse attraction, irradiation, and transformation of the influences are inhibited, such that those already produced and retained are swept away, and the patient achieves full recovery.

All diseases, therefore, originate from an accidental impression which darkens the spirits and displaces them from the perfection of their natural temperament <note>In historical medicine, "temperament" refers to the unique balance of vital forces or humors that constitutes an individual's health.</note> into a hostile or unnatural state. For this reason, all diseases can be cured by a remedy that brings them back to the perfection of their natural temperament through a natural counter-impression or radiant power <note>original: *Einstrahlungskraft*</note>. However, this can only occur so long as the hour appointed by the infinite wisdom of the Eternal Almighty for dissolution and decay <note>original: *Verwesung*</note> is still distant; because once the decree is made, it remains unalterable that all flesh must pass through decay. Furthermore, in the same proportion by which the spirits in their corrupted state produce unnatural effects—either in the whole body when they are thoroughly irritated, or, as the case may be, only in one part or another, from which the <term>symptoms <note>original: *Symptomata*</note></term> arise that characterize the disease—so too can these very spirits, in the opposite manner, as soon as they are pacified and brought back to the previous perfection of their natural temperament, dissolve, separate, and expel all <term>foreign substances <note>original: *heterogenea*</note></term> or <term>morbid matter <note>original: *materiam peccantem*; literally "sinning matter," a common early modern medical term for the physical substance—such as bad humors—thought to cause a disease</note></term> which were generated here or there by their unnatural effects, or left behind unseparated from the pure substances, and which cause the aforementioned symptoms. Thus, they [the spirits] enable themselves,

setzen, <note>Catchword: "to set" or "to enable," continuing to the next page.</note>

<summary>The author explains that disease is caused by external impressions that "darken" the body's vital spirits, and that healing occurs when a remedy restores these spirits to their natural state, provided the divinely appointed time of death has not arrived.</summary>

<term>Evacuants <note>original: *Evacuantia*; medicines used to clear the body of fluids, such as laxatives or emetics.</note></term>, <term>restringents <note>original: *restringentia*; substances that contract tissues or stop discharges.</note></term>, relaxants, diluents, and other such things can indeed be beneficial as long as the patient remains in good strength, the <term>materia peccans <note>Latin for "sinful matter," referring to the corrupted substance or "muck" thought to cause disease.</note></term> is still contained entirely <term>in primis viis <note>Latin for "in the first passages," meaning the stomach and intestinal tract.</note></term>, and the spirits <note>The "vital spirits" or internal life forces.</note> are not yet affected. Many a reader will have no concept of this radiation <note>original: *Einstrahlung*</note>. But whoever ripely considers how it happens, for example, that in a purified <term>health-cup <note>original: *Gesundheitsbecher*; specifically an "antimonial cup." Wine left in such a cup would absorb emetic properties from the antimony without the metal appearing to dissolve.</note></term> prepared from <term>antimony <note>original: *Spießglase*</note></term> and metals according to the rules of art <note>original: *l. a.*, an abbreviation for *lege artis*.</note>, many thousands of casks of wine can be gradually transformed into a sweat-inducing, or even a laxative or emetic remedy (according to one's desire)—or, on the other hand, how millions of measures of water can be turned into a worm-killing medicine by a single pound of liquid mercury—without either the said cup or the mercury losing even a single grain of its weight; such a person will necessarily understand and conclude that no dissolved matter from the cup enters the wine, nor from the mercury into the water. Instead, a mere impression of these wonderful natural bodies, which they make upon the aforementioned liquids by way of radiation, is the fundamental cause of the astonishing healing power they receive and demonstrate in this manner; and will

...from this, one can quite easily conclude the way in which our natural, animal, and life spirits <note>In early modern medicine, these are the three "spiritus" or vital forces: *natural* (governing nutrition), *animal* (governing senses and movement), and *vital* (governing heat and life).</note> can be injured or improved by a process of radiation <note>original: *einstrahlungswreise*; the way in which an influence "beams" or flows into something.</note>. Now, beloved reader! When you hold all this up against each other and consider it quite precisely, must not your own reason—your mere reason—completely convince you that our **matter**, as soon as it is brought to its perfection, is just such a concentrated **healing remedy** <note>original: *Heilmittel*</note>? It is a remedy that radiates into the spirits, preserves them against all corruption, and restores them to their natural perfection when they have been irritated and displaced from it, and consequently must surely heal almost all diseases.
